```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0001
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 449d-450c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 449d-450c
  start_marker: 449d
  end_marker: 450c
  start_char: 4716
  end_char: 6788
  text_sha256: 6c1e059e9b7f8ce135b2540200a6efe8c766182c8c262579a6bb9b7201780ffd
speaker: ΓΟΡ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Rhetoric is about speeches, and whereas the other arts' expertise is almost entirely about manual work and such activities, rhetoric has no such handiwork — its entire activity and accomplishment is through speeches.
greek_terms:
  - περὶ λόγους
  - χειρούργημα
  - χειρουργίας
  - πρᾶξις
  - κύρωσις
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 449e
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 449e
      start_marker: 449e
      end_marker: 449e
      start_char: 5183
      end_char: 5501
      text_sha256: 73c1fe412f14e22f849727a9cd15b52c8eafb5d4c63817889ea23998d9425860
  - kind: reaffirmed
    stephanus_span: 450b-450c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 450b-450c
      start_marker: 450b
      end_marker: 450c
      start_char: 5873
      end_char: 6788
      text_sha256: b4872633dc1c1e7bf5e0cd5223da8c5c4949f7a4c0a70311317adf720c5c04ef
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0469
limits: The claim does not yet specify what kind of speeches or about what subject — only that rhetoric operates entirely through speeches and not through manual work. The scope is explicitly deferred to further questioning.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0002
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 450d-450e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 450d-450e
  start_marker: 450d
  end_marker: 450e
  start_char: 6788
  end_char: 7692
  text_sha256: 97f0a6738378d8e4cf560498f0ab87c4eadc3741b249eaf154c8aa6314e26ff4
speaker: ΓΟΡ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Rhetoric is one of those arts that accomplish everything through speech, needing little or no manual work — a class including arithmetic, calculation, geometry, and draughts.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 450d-450e
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 450d-450e
      start_marker: 450d
      end_marker: 450e
      start_char: 6788
      end_char: 7692
      text_sha256: 97f0a6738378d8e4cf560498f0ab87c4eadc3741b249eaf154c8aa6314e26ff4
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0470
limits: This classification is per genus (speech-accomplished arts) but the differentia is still missing. Socrates explicitly notes that Gorgias would not call arithmetic or geometry 'rhetoric' despite their shared genus, showing the definition remains incomplete.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0003
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 451d

speaker: ΓΟΡ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Rhetoric is concerned with the greatest and best of human affairs.

  - τὰ μέγιστα τῶν ἀνθρωπείων πραγμάτων
  - ἄριστα
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 451d

      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 451d

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0004
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 451a-451c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 451a-451c
  start_marker: 451a
  end_marker: 451c
  start_char: 7692
  end_char: 9095
  text_sha256: 5eab0661df1ff483a1eed43c6d09bfeca6763cc3ebb42d7ca5c2e0c88eec4152
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: A proper definition of an art that accomplishes everything through speech must specify both the genus (speech-accomplished art) and the differentia (the particular subject matter about which the speeches are), as illustrated with arithmetic (about even and odd), calculation (about even and odd in relation to quantity), and astronomy (about the motion of stars, sun, and moon).
greek_terms:
  - γνῶσις
  - ἄρτιόν τε καὶ περιττὸν
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination
    stephanus_span: 451a-451c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 451a-451c
      start_marker: 451a
      end_marker: 451c
      start_char: 7692
      end_char: 9095
      text_sha256: 5eab0661df1ff483a1eed43c6d09bfeca6763cc3ebb42d7ca5c2e0c88eec4152
final_status: posed_only
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0472
limits: This is a method rule illustrated by analogy; it is not itself a definition of rhetoric but a framework for eliciting one. Gorgias does not contest the method.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0005
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 452d-452e

claim_kind: definition

  The greatest good for human beings, of which Gorgias is the craftsman, is the ability to persuade by speeches, exercising this power over judges in lawcourts, councilors in the council, assemblymen in the assembly, and in any other political gathering whatsoever.

  []

  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 452d-452e

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0006
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 452e

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0007
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 453a

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: definition
content: |
  Rhetoric is the craftsman of persuasion, and its whole business and sum culminates in producing persuasion in the souls of listeners.
greek_terms:
  - πειθοῦς δημιουργός
  - τὸ κεφάλαιον
stance_events:

    stephanus_span: 453a

This record does not establish that Socrates himself affirms this definition.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0010
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 453b-453c

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule

```

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0028

speaker: ΓΟΡ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Rhetoric, like all competitive arts (boxing, pankration, fighting in armour), is morally neutral; it is not the teacher or the art that is responsible when the practitioner uses it unjustly, but the practitioner who uses it wrongly.

  []

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0029
source_work: Gorgias

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: In dialectical discussion, disputants frequently become angry, accuse each other of speaking out of envy, engage in personal rivalry rather than seeking the matter at hand, and end in mutual abuse — but proper dialectic should consist in defining terms, learning from each other, and dissolving the discussion in an orderly way.
greek_terms:
  []

  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 457c-457e
    source_ref:

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0496
limits: This records Socrates's description of proper versus corrupted dialectical procedure. It does not establish that Socrates and Gorgias succeed in following the proper method.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0031

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis

  []

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0032
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 458b-458c

speaker: ΓΟΡ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: Gorgias accepts that he is the kind of person Socrates describes — one who is glad to be refuted if speaking falsely and glad to refute another if they speak falsely — but notes that the discussion should also take into account the wishes of those present, since he had already given a long display speech before Socrates arrived and a dialectical exchange might go on at length.
greek_terms:
  []

  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 458b-458c

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:

limits: This records Gorgias's acceptance of the dialectical ethos and his procedural concern about the audience. It does not establish that Gorgias will actually abide by the refutation-friendly ethos in practice.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```yaml

greek_terms:
  - δίκαιον
  - ἄδικον
  - αἰσχρὸν
  - καλὸν
  - ἀγαθὸν

```yaml

```yaml

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Socrates states that he initially understood from Gorgias's earlier statement that rhetoric is about speeches concerning the just and the unjust that rhetoric could never be an unjust thing, but then Gorgias said the rhetorician might use rhetoric unjustly, which seemed inconsistent; now, upon examination, it is agreed that the rhetorician cannot use rhetoric unjustly or wish to commit injustice.
greek_terms:
  - οὐ συνᾴδειν
  - ὁμολογεῖται
stance_events:

limits: Socrates presents this as the agreed outcome of the examination, but the chain depends on Gorgias's stepwise assents, several of which were qualified. The record does not establish that the contradiction is resolved for Gorgias.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

limits: This record does not assert that Gorgias's rhetoric is flattery; Socrates explicitly says at 463a he does not know whether Gorgias's practice is the same as what he calls rhetoric. The definition applies only to what Socrates himself calls rhetoric.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0059

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0061
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 464c-464d

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis

  - στοχασαμένη
  - ἡδίστῳ
  - ἄνοιαν

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0063
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 464e-465a

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Flattery is shameful because it aims at the pleasant without the best, and it is not a craft but a routine, because it has no account of the nature of what it applies or the cause of each thing.

  - αἰσχρόν

  - λόγον

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0064
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 465a-465b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: As cookery is the flattery that underlies medicine, so self-adornment underlies gymnastic — a mischievous, deceptive, ignoble, and illiberal thing that deceives through figures, colours, smoothness, and clothes, causing people to draw on alien beauty and neglect the proper beauty that comes through gymnastic.
greek_terms:
  - κομμωτική
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 465a-465b
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0530
limits: This record does not establish that self-adornment in fact operates this way; the claim is asserted within the flattery framework.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis

  - γυμναστική
  - νομοθετική

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0077
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 468c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 468c
  start_marker: 468c
  end_marker: 468c
  start_char: 42372
  end_char: 42776
  text_sha256: 5821bf2dda9ac4efd8e1fc3b50315a30d7f241a0a7c37c4f0c5ff46386b8df0a
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: We do not wish to kill, banish, or confiscate property simply as such, but only when such acts are beneficial; when they are harmful we do not wish them.
greek_terms:
  - βουλόμεθα
  - σφάττειν
  - ὠφέλιμα
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 468c

      stephanus_span: 468c
      start_marker: 468c

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0542
limits: Polus agrees this is true. The claim follows from the prior premises that all action is for the sake of the good and that we wish ends not means.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0080
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 469c

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis

  - ἀδικεῖσθαι

  - kind: asserted

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0081
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 469c-469e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 469c-469e

  end_char: 45709

speaker: ΠΩΛ.
claim_kind: definition
content: Tyrannical power consists in being able to do whatever seems best to one in the city, including killing, exiling, and doing everything according to one's own judgment.
greek_terms:
  - τυραννεῖν
  - ἐξεῖναι ἐν τῇ πόλει, ὃ ἂν δοκῇ αὐτῷ, ποιεῖν τοῦτο

    stephanus_span: 469c

observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0546
limits: Polus offers this as his definition of tyrannical rule, reiterating his earlier position. It does not record whether Socrates accepts this definition; Socrates explicitly distinguishes his own understanding of the term at 469c.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0082
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 469d-469e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 469d-469e

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Being able to do whatever seems best to one, without intelligence, is not truly great power; it is the kind of power anyone could have merely by carrying a dagger in the marketplace.

  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 469d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 469d

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0547
limits: Socrates uses a hypothetical scenario (the dagger in the marketplace) as an illustration; Polus concedes at 469e that this kind of power is not what he means. This record does not assert that Polus accepts Socrates' positive account.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0083
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 469e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

speaker: ΠΩΛ.
claim_kind: thesis

  - μέγα δύνασθαι

review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0084
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 470a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 470a

  text_sha256: a63d7004da67aab346a1f124eed137cd89cc04b200805146fa1285c0100e91b4
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If doing what seems best to one is accompanied by beneficial action, then great power is good and this is what great power consists in; but if not, it is bad and is only small power.
greek_terms:
  - μέγα δύνασθαι
  - ὠφελίμως πράττειν

  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 470a

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:

limits: Socrates presents this as what appears given the agreed premises; it is conditional on Polus' earlier concession that being penalized is bad. The record does not establish that Polus explicitly accepts this account of great power.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0087

observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0551
limits: Socrates states this as his own view. He does not claim to know whether the Great King or Archelaus is happy because he does not know their education and justice. This thesis is presented as the criterion for happiness; Polus has not yet conceded it.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0095
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 472a-472c

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: A refutation that relies on many witnesses is worthless for the truth, because one can be falsely testified against by many reputable-seeming people; the only valid refutation requires the interlocutor himself to serve as the single witness and to agree with what is said.
greek_terms:
  - ἀλήθειαν
  - μάρτυρας
  - ψευδομάρτυρας
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 472a-472c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 472a-472c
      start_marker: 472a
      end_marker: 472c
      start_char: 49904
      end_char: 51269
      text_sha256: 99b5a46b8af319506afae286f9201e7998935b6b2baa6b7fd2ffd7ee2c017b50
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0556
limits: This record does not establish that Socrates rejects all witness testimony for any purpose; the rule is about refutation specifically. It does not establish that this method rule is applied consistently throughout the dialogue. It does not assess whether Polus accepts this rule.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0127
source_work: Gorgias

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: There are three kinds of vice corresponding to three domains — poverty for possessions, disease for the body, and injustice for the soul — and injustice, taken together with the whole vice of soul, is the most shameful of them.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 477b-477c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0585
limits: The threefold classification is built through Polus's agreement at each step at 477b-c. The claim that soul-vice is most shameful is accepted by Polus but not independently argued.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```

```yaml

Polus assents.
review_status: accepted
```

content: Committing injustice is the second greatest of evils by magnitude; committing injustice and not paying the penalty is by nature the greatest and first of all evils.
greek_terms:
  - τὸ ἀδικεῖν
  - ἀδικοῦντα μὴ διδόναι δίκην
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 479d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 479d
      start_marker: 479d
      end_marker: 479d
      start_char: 65355
      end_char: 65777
      text_sha256: 08938454f000c5ded0a6377a7a2dcaa405c36c269e29a662b6364e9fc669bd8e
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0599
limits: Polus assents; the rank order is deduced from the prior premises (injustice is the greatest evil, punishment is release, non-punishment is retention) and does not independently defend those premises.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0143
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 479e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 479e
  start_marker: 479e
  end_marker: 479e
  start_char: 65777
  end_char: 66159
  text_sha256: 71902db12d4196cd136b09c30a0a59995a6d826a3ac7cafcce2f4d9a850b7efe
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Whoever commits injustice and does not pay the penalty — whether Archelaus or any other human — deserves to be exceptionally wretched, and the one who commits injustice is always more wretched than the one who suffers it, and the one who does not pay the penalty more wretched than the one who does.
greek_terms:
  - ἀθλίῳ
  - ἀθλιώτερον
stance_events:
  - kind: reaffirmed
    stephanus_span: 479e
    source_ref:

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0600
limits: Socrates recalls this as his own earlier thesis now proven; Polus concedes the demonstration. The record does not re-argue the proof.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0144
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 480a-480b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 480a-480b

  end_marker: 480b

  end_char: 66954
  text_sha256: dcb83e3aac2054085343a457bcf71208c976f0e83395fa59bf427f9564b1ecfc
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: On the basis of what has been agreed, one should above all guard against committing injustice, and if one does commit injustice, one should go willingly and quickly to the judge as to a doctor, so that the disease of injustice does not become chronic and make the soul incurable.
greek_terms:
  - μὴ ἀδικήσει
  - ἀνίατον
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 480a-480b
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0601
limits: The claim is a practical rule derived from the preceding argument; Polus does not dispute it in this segment, and it does not establish that this rule is binding on anyone who rejects the premises.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0145
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 480b

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis

  - οὐ χρήσιμος οὐδὲν
  - ῥητορικὴ

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0146

speaker: ΚΑΛ.

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0147
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 483a

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: "Socrates exploits the nature/convention opposition in argument: when someone speaks according to convention, Socrates cross-examines according to nature, and vice versa."

  - κατὰ νόμον
  - κατὰ φύσιν
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 483a

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0148
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 483a-483b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 483a-483b
  start_marker: 483a
  end_marker: 483b

  end_char: 73141
  text_sha256: 2f54a9491379c55187c9792dfd8552314fed61b47c8985ca4131f23e57895749
speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: By nature, whatever is worse is also more shameful, so suffering injustice is by nature more shameful than doing injustice, while by convention the reverse holds.
greek_terms:
  - αἴσχιον
  - κάκιον
  - τὸ ἀδικεῖσθαι
  - τὸ ἀδικεῖν
stance_events:

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0149
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 483b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 483b
  start_marker: 483b
  end_marker: 483b

speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Suffering injustice is not the experience of a real man but of a slave, for whom it is better to die than to live, being unable to defend himself when wronged or insulted.
greek_terms:
  - ἀνδρὸς
  - ἀνδραπόδου
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 483b

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0150
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 483b-483c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 483b-483c
  start_marker: 483b
  end_marker: 483c
  start_char: 72748
  end_char: 73590
  text_sha256: fc0de90864d35e45f49e301ae17e1342c0384fc8f51a931b05dbcfbca4803cc8
speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: report
content: The weak, who are the many, establish laws and assign praise and blame for their own advantage, frightening the stronger and more capable into not getting more than them.
greek_terms:
  - οἱ ἀσθενεῖς
  - οἱ πολλοί
  - πλέον ἔχειν
stance_events:
  - kind: reported
    stephanus_span: 483b-483c

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0607
limits: Callicles reports this as a general account of how conventional justice arises; he does not attribute it to a named thinker but presents it as his own analysis of the origin of nomos.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0151
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 483c-483d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 483c-483d
  start_marker: 483c
  end_marker: 483d
  start_char: 73141
  end_char: 73984
  text_sha256: ab3d87ea1a414dbcd6e50b8493ac6deae01f27825bc2df9076fe55a9d26303ca
speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: By convention, seeking to have more than the many is called unjust and shameful, but nature itself shows that it is just for the better and more powerful to have more than the worse and weaker.
greek_terms:
  - πλεονεκτεῖν
  - πλέον ἔχειν
  - δίκαιόν
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 483c-483d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 483c-483d

      end_marker: 483d

      end_char: 73984

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0608
limits: Callicles asserts this as a fact demonstrated everywhere in nature; the claim is not examined or challenged in this segment. It does not specify what 'better' and 'more powerful' mean.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0152
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 483d-483e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 483d-483e
  start_marker: 483d
  end_marker: 483e
  start_char: 73590
  end_char: 74330
  text_sha256: 0770e3ccd36480a535c71293b26583a47182ba8d437c0d2166d87477c1d6c83d
speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Nature's justice is visible in other animals and in whole cities and races of men: the stronger rules the weaker and has more."
greek_terms:
  - τὸ δίκαιον
  - τὸν κρείττω
  - τοῦ ἥττονος
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 483d-483e

final_status: left_standing

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0153
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 483e-484a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 483e-484a
  start_marker: 483e
  end_marker: 484a

speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: We mold the best and strongest among us from youth, like lion cubs, enslaving them through incantations by saying that equality is what is fine and just.
greek_terms:
  - πλάττοντες
  - λέοντας
  - κατεπᾴδοντές
  - καταδουλούμεθα
  - τὸ ἴσον

observation_ids:

limits: This extends the genealogy of conventional justice with a metaphor of domestication. Callicles speaks in the first-person plural as if describing a collective social practice, not a formal doctrine.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0154
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 484a-484b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 484a-484b
  start_marker: 484a

speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If a man arises with sufficient nature, he shakes off all these constraints, tramples our writings and spells and unnatural laws, and stands revealed as our master — the former slave — and there the justice of nature shines forth.
greek_terms:
  - ἀποσεισάμενος
  - διαρρήξας
  - διαφυγών
  - καταπατήσας
  - δεσπότης
  - τὸ τῆς φύσεως δίκαιον

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:

limits: This is a conditional thesis about what would happen if a naturally superior man appeared; it is not a claim that such a man currently exists or is identifiable.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0155
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 484b-484c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 484b-484c
  start_marker: 484b
  end_marker: 484c
  start_char: 74664
  end_char: 75561
  text_sha256: 0a679de0ed382db7105624431e6a01569c45d36b5bde158402018d7846bd60d3
speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: report
content: "Pindar indicates the same view in his song: law, king of all mortals and immortals, leads, making just the most violent act with highest hand, and as evidence he cites Heracles driving off Geryon's cattle without purchase or gift, on the ground that the cattle and all possessions of the worse belong by nature to the better and stronger."
greek_terms:
  - νόμος ὁ πάντων βασιλεὺς
  - δικαιῶν τὸ βιαιότατον
  - Ἡρακλέος
  - Γηρυόνου
stance_events:
  - kind: reported
    stephanus_span: 484b-484c
    source_ref:

      stephanus_span: 484b-484c

      end_marker: 484c

      end_char: 75561

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0612
limits: Callicles reports Pindar as supporting his own thesis about natural justice; he disclaims exact knowledge of the song. This is presented as corroboration, not as an independent argument.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0156
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 484c-484e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 484c-484e
  start_marker: 484c
  end_marker: 484e
  start_char: 75087
  end_char: 76345
  text_sha256: 91bbd35bddb5d3f90a8d156523f2bce535f37c8604569573a86652dc17c3dc88
speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "If someone pursues philosophy past the proper age, it corrupts him: he becomes inexperienced in the city's laws, in the speeches needed for public and private dealings, in human pleasures and desires, and in characters altogether, and when he enters any private or political action he becomes ridiculous."
greek_terms:
  - φιλοσοφία
  - διαφθορὰ
  - ἄπειρον
  - καταγέλαστοι
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 484c-484e
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 484c-484e
      start_marker: 484c
      end_marker: 484e

      end_char: 76345
      text_sha256: 91bbd35bddb5d3f90a8d156523f2bce535f37c8604569573a86652dc17c3dc88
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0613
limits: Callicles asserts this as advice to Socrates, not as a thesis posed for dialectical examination. It is a practical warning about the social consequences of prolonged philosophy, not a claim about the truth-value of philosophical inquiry.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0157
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 485a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 485a
  start_marker: 485a
  end_marker: 485a

speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: It is most correct to participate in both — philosophy to the extent appropriate for education, which is fine for a youth, but for an older man to continue philosophizing becomes ridiculous.

  - φιλοσοφίας μὲν ὅσον παιδείας χάριν
  - μειρακίῳ ὄντι
  - πρεσβύτερος ὢν ἄνθρωπος ἔτι φιλοσοφῇ, καταγέλαστον

    stephanus_span: 485a

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0614
limits: Callicles asserts this as his own view, not as a definition under Socratic examination; the segment does not show Socrates challenging this claim.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0158
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 485b-485c
source_ref:

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0160
source_work: Gorgias

  end_char: 78831
  text_sha256: 3070be30fabaafecac264020c0000ec1704b60eec201be15c6e8d39d2d8b046a
speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: report
content: Callicles reports that Euripides' Zethus reproached his brother Amphion for neglecting what he should care for, and Callicles applies the same reproach to Socrates — that Socrates neglects the things he should attend to and distorts his noble nature with a boyish guise, so that he could neither contribute a correct speech in councils of justice nor grasp something plausible and persuasive, nor devise a bold plan on another's behalf.
greek_terms:
  - ἀμελεῖς
stance_events:
  - kind: reported
    stephanus_span: 485e-486a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

      end_char: 78831

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0617
limits: This is a reported literary comparison, not a thesis under dialectical examination. The Euripidean quote is reported as the model for Callicles' own reproach.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: thesis

```yaml

```yaml

speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: One should stop refuting, practice the good order of practical affairs, and leave the clever subtle things to others — emulating not those who examine small matters but those who have life, reputation, and many other goods.

  - πραγμάτων δ’ εὐμουσίαν ἄσκει

```yaml

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: "Socrates proposes a method rule by analogy: just as one would seek the best touchstone to test whether gold is genuine, he believes he has found in Callicles a person against whom to test whether his soul is properly tended — if Callicles agrees with him, that will be sufficient proof."
greek_terms:
  - χρυσῆν ἔχων ἐτύγχανον τὴν ψυχήν
  - βασανίζουσιν τὸν χρυσόν
  - εὖ εἴσεσθαι ὅτι ἱκανῶς ἔχω

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```yaml

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Socrates asserts that Callicles possesses all three required qualities — knowledge, goodwill, and frankness — unlike Gorgias and Polus, who though knowledgeable and friendly are deficient in frankness and too ashamed, so much so that each dared to contradict himself before many people on the greatest matters.
greek_terms:

```

```yaml

content: "Socrates reports that Callicles, Teisander, Andron, and Nausicydes once deliberated and agreed on a doctrine: one should not be eager to pursue philosophy to the point of precision, but should be careful not to become wiser than is necessary lest one be corrupted unnoticed."
greek_terms:
  - μὴ προθυμεῖσθαι εἰς τὴν ἀκρίβειαν φιλοσοφεῖν
  - μὴ πέρα τοῦ δέοντος σοφώτεροι γενόμενοι λήσετε διαφθαρέντες

```

```yaml

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Socrates, recapitulating Callicles' and Pindar's position, asks whether the just by nature is that the stronger lead away the possessions of the weaker by force, the better rule the worse, and the superior have more than the inferior.
greek_terms:
  - τὸ δίκαιον
  - τὸ κατὰ φύσιν
  - ἄγειν βίᾳ τὸν κρείττω τὰ τῶν ἡττόνων

final_status: posed_only

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0182
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 489a-489b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 489a-489b
  start_marker: 489a
  end_marker: 489b
  start_char: 84448
  end_char: 85303
  text_sha256: 205e29d17ce6d9c20040e0f6951522d5942e75f8c28ce59f0a4a3c94dbb0f992
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Socrates infers that it is not only by convention that committing injustice is more shameful than suffering it, nor that having an equal share is just, but also by nature — and therefore Callicles was wrong to say that convention and nature are opposed.
greek_terms:
  - οὐ νόμῳ ἄρα μόνον
  - ἀλλὰ καὶ φύσει
  - κινδυνεύεις οὐκ ἀληθῆ λέγειν
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 489a-489b

final_status: left_standing

  - obs_gorgias_0639
limits: This inference depends on Callicles' earlier concessions that stronger = better and that the many are stronger; the record does not establish that Socrates himself endorses the conclusion independently of those premises.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0184
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 489d

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Socrates states that Callicles surely does not think two are better than one, or his own slaves better than himself, just because they are physically stronger.
greek_terms:
  - οὐ γὰρ δήπου σύ γε τοὺς δύο βελτίους ἡγῇ τοῦ ἑνός
  - οὐδὲ τοὺς σοὺς δούλους βελτίους σοῦ

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0186
source_work: Gorgias

speaker: ΣΩ.

  - δηλοῖς δὲ οὐδέν

final_status: left_standing

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0187

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0188
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 490a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 490a
  start_marker: 490a
  end_marker: 490a
  start_char: 86510
  end_char: 86923

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: On the thesis that the better and stronger are the more intelligent, one intelligent man is stronger than ten thousand unintelligent men, and the one should rule and have more than the many.
greek_terms:
  - εἷς φρονῶν
  - μυρίων μὴ φρονούντων
  - πλέον ἔχειν

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0189

speaker: ΚΑΛ.

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0190
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 490b-490c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 490b-490c
  start_marker: 490b
  end_marker: 490c

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The more intelligent concerning food and drink — a doctor — is better and stronger regarding those things, but should distribute rather than take more for himself, and if weakest, should take least.
greek_terms:
  - φρονιμώτερος
  - βελτίων
  - κρείττων
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0192
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 491b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 491b
  start_marker: 491b
  end_marker: 491b
  start_char: 88971
  end_char: 89393
  text_sha256: fc59881cef4ce32ceed78d57e7087a8a5e642bd5eac8ccddd9dc1739a610eceb
speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: definition
content: The stronger are those who are intelligent concerning the affairs of the city — knowing how the city would be well governed — and who are also courageous, capable of accomplishing what they intend and not giving up through softness of soul.
greek_terms:
  - φρόνιμοι
  - εἰς τὰ τῆς πόλεως πράγματα
  - εὖ οἰκοῖτο
  - ἀνδρεῖοι
  - μαλακίαν τῆς ψυχῆς
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 491b
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 491b
      start_marker: 491b
      end_marker: 491b
      start_char: 88971
      end_char: 89393
      text_sha256: fc59881cef4ce32ceed78d57e7087a8a5e642bd5eac8ccddd9dc1739a610eceb
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0649
limits: This is Callicles' definitive positive specification of who the stronger are, but it merges intelligence and courage without addressing whether one quality suffices without the other.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0193
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 491c

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: "Socrates charges that Callicles has defined the better and stronger inconsistently: first as the physically stronger, then as the more intelligent, and now as the more courageous; he demands a stable account of who the better and stronger are and in respect of what."
greek_terms:
  - ἰσχυροτέρους
  - φρονιμωτέρους
  - ἀνδρειότεροί
  - εἰς ὅτι
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 491c

      end_char: 89766
      text_sha256: fca9d64c43f923cf6f4f5620e7b87c94c76ff9b926d57ca0e7f61764aee6da60
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0650
limits: Socrates does not assert that Callicles has contradicted himself; he identifies instability across definitions. The charge of variation is itself checkable from the text but does not prove Callicles has no consistent view.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0194
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 491d

speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: definition
content: "The better and stronger are those intelligent in city affairs and courageous; it is fitting that these rule the cities, and this is justice: that the rulers have more than the ruled."

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0197
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 491e-492a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "What is by nature fine and just is this: the man who intends to live correctly should let his desires grow as large as possible and not restrain them, and being capable through courage and intelligence, should serve and fill those desires whenever they arise."
greek_terms:
  - τὸ κατὰ φύσιν καλὸν καὶ δίκαιον
  - τὸν ὀρθῶς βιωσόμενον
  - μὴ κολάζειν

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0654
limits: This is Callicles' positive hedonist-naturalist doctrine, offered with explicit frankness. It is asserted, not yet examined or refuted in this segment. The text breaks off mid-sentence at 492a.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0198
source_work: Gorgias

speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: report
content: The many are incapable of filling their desires and therefore blame the intemperate out of shame, concealing their own incapacity, calling intemperance shameful — thereby enslaving those who are better by nature and praising temperance because they themselves cannot furnish fulfillment for their pleasures.
greek_terms:
  - τοῖς πολλοῖς
  - ἀδυναμίαν
  - ἀκολασίαν
  - τοὺς βελτίους τὴν φύσιν
  - σωφροσύνην

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```yaml

  - ἐπιθυμίαι
  - πίθον

  - ἀπληστίαν
  - Ἅιδου
  - κόσκινον

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0234

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The good is the end of all actions, and everything else must be done for the sake of the good, not the good for the sake of other things.

  []

  - kind: reaffirmed
    stephanus_span: 499e-500a

observation_ids:

limits: This record does not establish the content of the good, only its formal role as a final end. Socrates references it as a point already agreed with Polus and now confirmed by Callicles.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0237

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: A distinction was earlier drawn between pursuits aimed only at pleasure without knowledge of better or worse, and those that know what is good and bad; cookery was classed as experience, while medicine was classed as an art.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: reaffirmed
    stephanus_span: 500a-500b
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

observation_ids:

limits: This record does not establish or assess the cookery/medicine distinction itself; it records that Socrates recalls and reaffirms this earlier method rule.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0238
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 500e-501b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 500e-501b
  start_marker: 500e
  end_marker: 501b
  start_char: 108727
  end_char: 109804
  text_sha256: 8ba1d921303b4eb3e584dff69f35238781d8dab0575630ce64d3a2008f2d9a67
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: An art investigates the nature and cause of what it attends to and can give an account of each thing it does; a mere experience proceeds without art, investigating neither the nature nor the cause of its object, preserving only by practice and experience the memory of what customarily happens.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: reaffirmed
    stephanus_span: 500e-501b
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 500e-501b
      start_marker: 500e
      end_marker: 501b

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0691
limits: This record does not establish that this criterion for distinguishing art from experience is exhaustive or that Socrates' examples exhaust the class of arts.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0239
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 501b-501c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "There are pursuits concerning the soul analogous to those concerning the body: some are artful, having forethought for what is best for the soul, while others neglect this and attend only to the soul's pleasure, caring for nothing but gratification regardless of whether it is better or worse."
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 501b-501c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 501b-501c
      start_marker: 501b
      end_marker: 501c

      end_char: 110220

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0692
limits: This record does not identify which soul-pursuits are artful and which are mere gratification; Socrates poses the distinction for Callicles' consideration.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0251
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 503a-503b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 503a-503b
  start_marker: 503a
  end_marker: 503b
  start_char: 113157
  end_char: 114006
  text_sha256: 7016462abe2b984d13155d805b51b77a6598fb5d199ec46b23a993b0bda4e538
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Rhetoric is twofold: one kind is flattery and shameful public speaking, the other is noble and strives to make the citizens' souls as good as possible, fighting to say what is best whether it is pleasant or unpleasant."
greek_terms:
  - διπλοῦν
  - κολακεία
  - αἰσχρὰ δημηγορία
  - καλόν
  - διαμάχεσθαι λέγοντα τὰ βέλτιστα
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 503a-503b

      end_char: 114006

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0703
limits: This record does not establish that this noble rhetoric actually exists in practice — Socrates immediately questions whether Callicles has ever seen it.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0255
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 503c-503d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 503c-503d
  start_marker: 503c
  end_marker: 503d
  start_char: 114006
  end_char: 114775
  text_sha256: df64a3cae9d5f834a3b25291715177374078301f46af62955db7999100c8c4a6
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: "Whether Themistocles, Cimon, Miltiades, and Pericles were good men depends on which definition of virtue is used: if virtue is filling appetites, one judgment follows; if virtue is the craft of fulfilling only the appetites that make a person better, another judgment follows."
greek_terms:
  - τὰς ἐπιθυμίας ἀποπιμπλάναι
  - τέχνη τις
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 503c-503d

      end_char: 114775

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0707
limits: This record does not settle which definition of virtue is correct — only that the evaluation of these men depends on which definition is adopted.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0256
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 503e-504b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 503e-504b

  start_char: 114775
  end_char: 115960
  text_sha256: 0c246b220f20cb075e65eb5f2423781ce2acec2cf095ce38f072c17c78e4f8d6
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: A house, ship, body, or soul that achieves order and arrangement is good; one that achieves disorder is bad.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination
    stephanus_span: 503e-504b
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 503e-504b
      start_marker: 503e
      end_marker: 504b
      start_char: 114775
      end_char: 115960
      text_sha256: 0c246b220f20cb075e65eb5f2423781ce2acec2cf095ce38f072c17c78e4f8d6
final_status: posed_only
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0708
limits: Does not specify what order and arrangement consist in for the soul; only states that the soul, like crafted objects and the body, becomes good through order. The claim proceeds by questioning Callicles at 503e-504a and is conceded by him as following necessarily from the preceding craft analogies at 504b.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0257
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 504c-504d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 504c-504d
  start_marker: 504c
  end_marker: 504d
  start_char: 115960
  end_char: 116864
  text_sha256: e4442e6872843e511be34bc98af9235bb4b934e87e1ba87174725344f79b0ecd
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: definition
content: The order and arrangement of the soul is called lawful and law, and this is justice and moderation.
greek_terms:
  - νόμος
  - δικαιοσύνη
  - σωφροσύνη
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination
    stephanus_span: 504c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 504c
      start_marker: 504c
      end_marker: 504c

      end_char: 116413
      text_sha256: 8cd0a3493f032ccfa14a0ca05727e3088d88911494f74240b6e445d10d9af1fb
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 504c-504d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 504c-504d
      start_marker: 504c
      end_marker: 504d
      start_char: 115960
      end_char: 116864
      text_sha256: e4442e6872843e511be34bc98af9235bb4b934e87e1ba87174725344f79b0ecd
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0709
limits: Defines justice and moderation as the soul's order but does not define what that order consists in. The naming proceeds by analogy with the body, where order produces health. Callicles concedes at 504d without examination.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0258

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The skilled and good orator aims at producing justice, moderation, and virtue in the souls of citizens and removing injustice, licentiousness, and vice, whether through words, actions, giving, or taking away.
greek_terms:
  - ῥήτωρ
  - δικαιοσύνη
  - σωφροσύνη
  - ἀδικία
  - ἀκολασία
  - κακία

final_status: left_standing

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0259
source_work: Gorgias

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Having a bad body makes life not worth living and one necessarily lives badly with a bad body, so giving pleasant things to a sick body without benefit is worse than useless.

  []

  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 505a
    source_ref:

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0264
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 505e-506a

  text_sha256: be8d78fa0a90feb08b721b47366085d89c51ebc26baa0b1cd6e47092c0a275a3
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: All participants should be eager to know what is true and false about the subject under discussion, for it is a common good that the truth become clear to all; Socrates himself does not speak from knowledge but seeks together with the others and will be the first to concede if shown to be wrong.
greek_terms:
  - ψεῦδος
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 505e-506a
    source_ref:

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:

limits: Stated as Socrates transitions to monologue after Callicles' withdrawal at 505d. Does not specify how truth is to be recognized or what standard of refutation applies. Socrates' disclaimer of knowledge at 506a is presented as part of the method, not as a thesis about his own epistemic state.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```yaml

claim_kind: thesis
content: The greatest evil is injustice to the doer, and an even greater evil, if possible, is committing injustice and not paying the penalty.

```

```yaml

  text_sha256: 4026eed0236f56403574dd32277966ffe6239708b957d3991001532a63ed5a84
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The only friend worth mentioning for such a tyrant is one of the same character who praises and blames the same things and is willing to be ruled by and subject to the ruler; this man will have great power in the city and no one will harm him with impunity.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 510c-510d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 510c-510d

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0728
limits: Limited to the case of a savage and uneducated tyrant; describes a prudential path to power and safety, not a normative endorsement.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0282
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 511c-511d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Rhetoric in the law courts is no more worthy of esteem than swimming or the pilot's art, since each of these saves people from death.
greek_terms:
  - ῥητορικὴν τὴν ἐν τοῖς δικαστηρίοις διασῴζουσαν
  - ἡ τοῦ νεῖν ἐπιστήμη
  - τὴν κυβερνητικήν

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0732
limits: Socrates uses a provocative comparison. Callicles concedes swimming is not held in honor, which Socrates exploits. The thesis is asserted and developed through the pilot analogy but not fully tested by Callicles' responses.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0283

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0284
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 512d-512e

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The noble and the good may be something other than saving oneself and being saved; the truly real man should not cling to life but should instead consider how to live as well as possible for whatever time he does live.
greek_terms:
  - τὸ σῴζειν τε καὶ σῴζεσθαι
  - φιλοψυχητέον

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0285

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0286
source_work: Gorgias

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis

```

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0288
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 513d

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0290
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 513e-514a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: In tending to the city and citizens, the practice must aim at making the citizens as good as possible; without this, no other benefaction is of any use unless the recipients' intellect is noble and good.
greek_terms:
  - καλὴ κἀγαθὴ ἡ διάνοια

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0291
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 514a-514d

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: Before undertaking public works in a craft such as building, one must examine whether one knows the craft, from whom one learned it, and whether one has previously built private works of quality.

  - τέχνην
  - οἰκοδομικήν
  - διδασκάλους

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```

```yaml

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0294
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 517e-518a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 517e-518a

  text_sha256: 36486704477651c969aec5e39c3bb856471d98fbb42cbddbe4a1d3eb3bd85521
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The genuine arts of body-care are distinct from and properly rule over subordinate body-oriented crafts like baking, cookery, weaving, and shoemaking, because the genuine arts know what is good and bad for the body's excellence while the subordinate crafts do not.
greek_terms:
  - γυμναστική
  - ἰατρική
  - σώματος θεραπεία
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 517e-518a

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0744
limits: The record establishes a craft hierarchy claim about body-care; it does not establish that this hierarchy applies to soul-care without the separate argument Socrates makes at 518a.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0295
source_work: Gorgias

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0296
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 518b-518c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 518b-518c
  start_marker: 518b
  end_marker: 518c
  start_char: 144363
  end_char: 145189
  text_sha256: 7eb15eee9e3f43f36748a358b19ddcbc58c9062133a8c408f6d932d36d82866b
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Callicles' praise of famous Athenian statesmen as good citizens is like praising bakers, cooks, and wine-merchants as excellent body-therapists — the figures he names are mere servants and preparers of desires, not genuine practitioners of the political art.
greek_terms:
  - διακόνους
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 518b-518c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 518b-518c
      start_marker: 518b
      end_marker: 518c
      start_char: 144363
      end_char: 145189
      text_sha256: 7eb15eee9e3f43f36748a358b19ddcbc58c9062133a8c408f6d932d36d82866b
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0746
limits: The record establishes that Socrates analogically reduces Callicles' political exemplars to desire-gratification servants; it does not establish that the named statesmen are in fact only such servants — that is the contested claim.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0297
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 518c-518d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 518c-518d
  start_marker: 518c
  end_marker: 518d
  start_char: 144809
  end_char: 145593
  text_sha256: b179f438187c204c8266bf316670c013121221222679f0a2ec6ebb96b3a08700
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Desire-gratifiers who merely fill and fatten bodies without knowledge of the healthy ultimately destroy even the original flesh, yet they are praised while the later-present advisers are blamed for the resulting illnesses.
greek_terms:
  - ὑγιεινοῦ
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 518c-518d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 518c-518d
      start_marker: 518c
      end_marker: 518d
      start_char: 144809
      end_char: 145593
      text_sha256: b179f438187c204c8266bf316670c013121221222679f0a2ec6ebb96b3a08700
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0747
limits: The record establishes the claim that gratification without health-knowledge is destructive and that blame is misdirected; it does not independently establish that the historical statesmen produced the same effect on Athens — that application follows in the next span.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0298
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 518e-519a
source_ref:

  text_sha256: bf53261ce173a11aebc92894ca1353df110dc83eb76827a97c4a414a18bb6e8f
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The older Athenian statesmen filled the city with harbors, dockyards, walls, tribute, and such nonsense without temperance and justice, making the city swollen and festering, though people say they made the city great.
greek_terms:
  - σωφροσύνης
  - δικαιοσύνης
  - φλυαριῶν
  - οἰδεῖ
  - ὕπουλός

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:

limits: The record establishes that Socrates diagnoses Athens as materially stuffed but morally hollow under the older statesmen; it does not establish the factual historical accuracy of this diagnosis.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0299
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 519a-519b

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: When the eventual crisis of the illness comes, the Athenians will blame the advisers present at that time while praising Themistocles, Cimon, and Pericles — the very causes of the evils — and may also go after Callicles and Alcibiades, who are not causes but perhaps joint-causes.
greek_terms:
  []

    stephanus_span: 519a-519b

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0749
limits: The record establishes Socrates' predictive-analytical framework for how Athens will misattribute blame in a future crisis; it does not establish the predicted events as fact.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0300
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 519b-519c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 519b-519c
  start_marker: 519b

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: No political leader of a city is ever destroyed unjustly by the very city he leads — the claim that such leaders suffer unjust destruction is false.

  - προστάτης

  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 519b-519c

observation_ids:

limits: The record establishes a strong general thesis about just deserts of political leadership; it does not establish how this thesis is grounded in the preceding craft hierarchy argument, nor does it name specific historical counterexamples.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0301
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 519c-519d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 519c-519d
  start_marker: 519c
  end_marker: 519d
  start_char: 146697
  end_char: 147508
  text_sha256: 1ac07c00fb9217a34a1b84f204df3b77513f84852aa39fd0f1af3f93f9c323a2
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Those who claim to be political leaders and the sophists are essentially the same — for sophists, though otherwise clever, do the absurd thing of claiming to teach virtue while then accusing their students of injustice, an impossibility if the students had truly acquired justice and been freed from injustice.
greek_terms:
  - σοφισταί
  - δικαιοσύνην
  - ἀδικίαν
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 519c-519d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 519c-519d
      start_marker: 519c
      end_marker: 519d
      start_char: 146697
      end_char: 147508
      text_sha256: 1ac07c00fb9217a34a1b84f204df3b77513f84852aa39fd0f1af3f93f9c323a2
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0751
limits: The record establishes Socrates' assimilation of political pretenders to sophists via a shared performative contradiction; it does not establish that all politicians or all sophists in fact face this contradiction, nor does it specify the full argument for their identity.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0302
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 519e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 519e
  start_marker: 519e

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: It is unreasonable for someone who claims to have made another person good to then find fault with that person for being wicked — the complaint contradicts the claimed success.

  - ἄλογον
  - πονηρός
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination

final_status: posed_only
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0752

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0303
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 520a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 520a
  start_marker: 520a
  end_marker: 520a
  start_char: 147869
  end_char: 148256
  text_sha256: 33ef2cadc8f13f8d86390e6b4a96391477bf4d0a949122ec4ae57180163d87cd
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Those who claim to lead the city and care for it so it becomes as excellent as possible but then, when it suits them, accuse it of being most wicked are no different from the sophists — the sophist and the rhetor are the same thing, or nearly so and closely akin.
greek_terms:
  - πονηροτάτης
  - ῥήτωρ
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 520a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt

      start_marker: 520a

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0753
limits: The record establishes the concluding identification of sophist and rhetor via a parallel performative contradiction; it does not establish that this identity holds in all respects — Socrates hedges with nearly so and closely akin.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0304
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 520b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 520b
  start_marker: 520b
  end_marker: 520b

  end_char: 148681

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Sophistic is more admirable than rhetoric to the degree that legislation surpasses judging and gymnastics surpasses medicine.
greek_terms:
  - σοφιστικὴ
  - ῥητορικῆς
  - νομοθετικὴ
  - δικαστικῆς
  - γυμναστικὴ
  - ἰατρικῆς

  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 520b

      stephanus_span: 520b
      start_marker: 520b
      end_marker: 520b

      end_char: 148681
      text_sha256: f15ddac75295b8ea7ff67406b95ea5d62c4167d8af4b145e55799da5f3580f66
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0754
limits: Does not establish that Socrates considers sophistic admirable in itself; the comparison is relative. The thesis is stated as Socrates' own view but is not cross-examined in this segment.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0305
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 520b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 520b
  start_marker: 520b
  end_marker: 520b
  start_char: 148256
  end_char: 148681
  text_sha256: f15ddac75295b8ea7ff67406b95ea5d62c4167d8af4b145e55799da5f3580f66
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Only popular orators and sophists cannot consistently blame the activity they teach for being bad for them, since that would be to accuse themselves of having provided no benefit to those they claim to benefit.
greek_terms:
  - δημηγόροις
  - σοφισταῖς
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 520b
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 520b
      start_marker: 520b
      end_marker: 520b
      start_char: 148256
      end_char: 148681
      text_sha256: f15ddac75295b8ea7ff67406b95ea5d62c4167d8af4b145e55799da5f3580f66
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0755
limits: Does not assert that all orators and sophists do blame their activity; only that they are the only ones for whom it is inconsistent to do so. Callicles' agreement is at 520c but the thesis is asserted by Socrates at 520b.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0306
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 520c-520d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 520c-520d
  start_marker: 520c
  end_marker: 520d
  start_char: 148681
  end_char: 149477
  text_sha256: 6d74179426a811ef199cae43bffa884de7e40bea38c97b5d90ea89df044ae6c2
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Only those who can make others good can safely bestow their benefit without pay, since removing injustice from a person removes any risk of being wronged by that person.
greek_terms:
  - ἀδικίαν
  - εὐεργεσίαν
  - ἀγαθοὺς ποιεῖν
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 520c-520d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 520c-520d
      start_marker: 520c
      end_marker: 520d
      start_char: 148681
      end_char: 149477
      text_sha256: 6d74179426a811ef199cae43bffa884de7e40bea38c97b5d90ea89df044ae6c2
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0756
limits: The thesis is conditional on the premise that someone can truly make others good (520d). It also assumes that injustice is the only source of wrongdoing. Callicles assents but does not examine the premise.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0307
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 520e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 520e
  start_marker: 520e
  end_marker: 520e
  start_char: 149477
  end_char: 149949
  text_sha256: 6d6c0afe741a6c54d40d4722a1dbc0dde4787fceb2e0467f7f783aa9926ba5c6
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Among all benefits, only making someone good produces in the recipient a desire to return the benefit, so that receiving a return benefit is a sign that the benefit was genuine.
greek_terms:
  - εὐεργεσιῶν
  - εὖ παθόντα
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 520e
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 520e
      start_marker: 520e
      end_marker: 520e

      end_char: 149949
      text_sha256: 6d6c0afe741a6c54d40d4722a1dbc0dde4787fceb2e0467f7f783aa9926ba5c6
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0757
limits: The thesis explains a social norm (charging fees for moral advice is shameful) rather than standing as an independently defended claim. Callicles assents but does not challenge it.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0308
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 521a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 521a
  start_marker: 521a
  end_marker: 521a
  start_char: 149949
  end_char: 150331
  text_sha256: fb6a2307f8bf81d3e49a85a82ece65517d860100f746e06de7e61ed97c32f6e6
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "There are two modes of caring for the city: fighting to make the citizens as good as possible (like a doctor), or serving and speaking to please them."
greek_terms:
  - θεραπείαν τῆς πόλεως
  - διαμάχεσθαι
  - διακονήσοντα
  - πρὸς χάριν
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination
    stephanus_span: 521a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 521a
      start_marker: 521a
      end_marker: 521a
      start_char: 149949
      end_char: 150331
      text_sha256: fb6a2307f8bf81d3e49a85a82ece65517d860100f746e06de7e61ed97c32f6e6
final_status: posed_only
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0758
limits: Socrates poses the dichotomy as a question to Callicles, asking which mode Callicles summons him to. The thesis itself is not examined here; only Callicles' answer is obtained.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

speaker: ΚΑΛ.
claim_kind: report
content: Callicles says he summons Socrates to serve the city, not to fight to make the citizens as good as possible.

  - διακονήσοντα

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0310
source_work: Gorgias
stephanus_span: 521d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
  stephanus_span: 521d
  start_marker: 521d

  end_char: 151581
  text_sha256: 6202e1144b1914da086c45cc7bb8fe25f82fbbd0ce1717e862090050082c922a
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Socrates claims that he is among a few Athenians who attempt the truly political art and that he alone among his contemporaries practices politics in this way.
greek_terms:
  - ὡς ἀληθῶς πολιτικῇ τέχνῃ
  - πράττειν τὰ πολιτικὰ
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 521d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/gorgias.txt
      stephanus_span: 521d
      start_marker: 521d

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_gorgias_0760
limits: Socrates hedges with 'I think' and 'lest I say alone' (521d). This is a self-characterization, not a thesis examined by Callicles in this segment. The claim defines what the true political art consists in negatively, by speaking for the best rather than for pleasure, but does not fully define it.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_gorgias_0311

  - ἰατρὸς
  - ὀψοποιοῦ
  - παῖδες
  - ὑγιεινῶς

review_status: accepted

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml

```yaml

limits: Socrates presents this as a practical consequence of the undisturbed argument, not as a thesis newly defended in this segment. The record does not establish that Callicles accepts this use of rhetoric.
review_status: accepted
```
